## 3. How to use it, step by step (and how to take it off)

Luxe's own instructions are only "2 coats. That's all it took" and "Warm water. That's it." [M] **S1**. Below is the full method, built from makeup artists, eye doctors and the tubing brands. Where advice differs, both sides are shown.

### 3.1 What you need

| Item | Why | Must-have? |
|---|---|---|
| The mascara | — | Yes |
| A mirror you can put **below** your face, ideally a magnifying one (5x–10x) | Looking down stops you hitting the lid; magnification helps tired eyes | Strongly advised over 50 |
| Good light from the front (a window) | Shadows hide clumps | Yes |
| Cotton buds (Q-tips) | Fixing smudges | Yes |
| Eyelash curler (optional) | Lifts straight lashes | Optional |
| Clean spoolie or lash comb (optional) | Separating clumps | Optional |
| Eyeshadow primer or loose powder (optional) | Stops oily or hooded lids smudging mascara | Optional, useful for hooded lids |

### 3.2 Step by step: applying it

1. **Start with clean, dry, cream-free lashes.**
   - Wash your face. Keep eye cream **below** the eye and away from the lashes, and let it sink in.
   - Why: oil or cream on lashes breaks mascara down and causes clumps and smudging. Nina Ubhi: "if you have like skin care left over on your lashes… it's actually oil, right? So, it's moisture and it's going to end up causing your mascara to slide off, flake, or break down" [M] **Y7**.
   - Dermatologist Dr Dray: wash lids before putting on new eye makeup, because "pollen dirt pollutants" settle there [M] **Y12**.
2. **(Hooded or oily lids) Prime or powder the lid.**
   - Put a tiny amount of eyeshadow primer on the lid, "especially right there, that's exactly where your lashes are going to touch", and wait about 30 seconds [M] **Y9**.
   - Or dust loose powder there. Commenter: "The key for preventing smudging (for me anyway) is to make sure the eye area is powdered" (@Pamela-B, **Y9** comments) [M].
3. **(Optional) Curl your lashes, BEFORE mascara.**
   - Why before: "curl your lashes gently before the mascara goes on, never after it dries because clamping a curler onto a coated lash is how brittle lashes break" [M] **Y2**.
   - How (classic): open the curler, slide it as close to the root as is comfortable without pinching skin, squeeze gently, then move out a little and squeeze again, then once more near the middle, so the lash curves like a C, not a sharp "check mark" [M] **Y3**, **Y9**.
   - How (tips-first, from makeup artist Erin Parsons via Michelle Spieler): curl the **tips** first, then the **middle**, then the **base** [M] **Y3**.
   - Pinching means the curler's curve doesn't match your eye; try another shape: "if you're constantly giving a little [flinch]… you don't have the right curve" [M] **Y9**.
   - Heated curler alternative: used **after** mascara, held on the lashes; no pinching (makeup artist Aran P, **Y18**) [M].
   - Note the warnings: Dr Dray says curlers often contain **nickel** (a common allergy) and can break lashes; clean them with an alcohol pad [M] **Y12**. A study of 36 women found curler use did **not** significantly change lash structure [M] **S14**.
4. **Take the wand out the right way. Do NOT pump it.**
   - Twist and pull out slowly. Wipe the extra off on the tube's neck, not on a tissue (saves product) [M] **Y18**.
   - "Don't pump it… rummage it around on the inside" [M] **Y9**. Pumping "pushes air in there into the barrel and it dries out the mascara so you're going to get clumps" [M] **Y18**. Thrive: "avoid pumping the wand in and out of the tube" [M] **S7**.
5. **Position: chin up, eyes down.**
   - Put the mirror below you and look down into it. "when you're looking down you're less likely to get it on your lids" [M] **Y9**. "tilt my head down so I'm looking up my nose" [M] **Y18**.
6. **First coat, upper lashes: wiggle at the root, then pull through.**
   - Put the brush at the very base of the lashes, wiggle side to side a little, then sweep up and out to the tips. Dominique Sachse: "Kind of the little wiggle. Little wiggle and then pull. And I feel like it just deposits a little bit more of the product at the base" [M] **Y6**.
   - Thrive: "Beginning at the base of your lashes, gently guide the wand from root to tip" [M] **S7**.
   - Use the **tip of the wand** held upright for the tiny inner- and outer-corner lashes [M] **Y10**, **Y6**.
   - A salon trick: brush the **tips** first with the wand tip, then do the root-to-tip pass, "you can never get the tips as long when you skip this step" (@DreamJeanneee1111, 56 likes, **Y17** comments) [M].
7. **Second coat: timing depends on the formula.**
   - **Tubing:** add the second coat **while the first is still wet**. Thrive: "Layer additional coats while lashes are still wet"; Tarte (per Dominique): "quickly apply the second coat before the first coat dries" [M] **S7**, **Y6**. A Thrive fan: "If it gets dry and then you add a coat it is more prone to clumping" (@CarolZone8A) [M].
   - Some tubing formulas are meant as one coat: makeup artist: "they are one coat only… anytime someone has gone in with the second or third coat they'll say oh it's really chunky" [M] **Y3**. Blinc's newer formula says you can build [M] **Y10**.
   - **Regular:** let it sit a few seconds, but not dry. Nina Ubhi: "Just let it sit a few seconds before you go in with the second coat… not even semi-dry" [M] **Y7**. Mindful Midlife: "don't ever let one coat completely dry" [M] **Y9**.
   - [R] Luxe says "2 coats". Do the second coat within a minute, as for tubing, until you know how it behaves.
8. **One eye at a time.** "For optimal performance, we recommend focusing on one eye at a time" [M] **S7**.
9. **Lower lashes: go light, or skip.**
   - Film and TV makeup artists won't put mascara on women's lower lashes, as it can "cast shadows and can end up making you look more hollowed out", especially over 40 [M] **Y3**.
   - "A light hand up top, almost nothing on the bottom" [M] **Y2**.
   - If you do them: wait until the top is dry, look up a little, and **dab** or use the wand **upright** (vertical) [M] **Y9**, **Y18**; "I use the mascara brush pointed up and down rather than sideways on bottom lashes" (@LisaIsHappyToBeSane, 119 likes) [M].
   - Tissue trick: hold a tissue under the lower lashes while doing the top (@granjmy, 82 likes) [M].
   - Some use a tubing mascara only on the lower lashes because it won't smear: "works great on the lower lashes as it doesn't smear, cause raccoon eyes, or fallout" (**fallout** = bits dropping off) (@hiannahgus574, 45 likes, **Y17c**) [M].
10. **Fix mistakes the safe way.**
    - Let a smudge on the lid **dry**, then flick it off with a dry cotton bud.
    - Comb clumps with a clean spoolie or lash comb. **Never a pin or safety pin**: Dr Dray, "you could inadvertently stab your eye" [M] **Y12**. (Some viral hacks use tweezers coated in mascara for lower lashes, **Y17**; [R] for shaky hands or poor sight, skip it.)
11. **Never apply in a moving car or bus.** FDA: a bump can scratch the eye with the wand, and "Even a slight scratch can result in a serious infection" [M] **S9**.

### 3.3 Step by step: taking it off

**Tubing (which is how Luxe describes itself)**
1. **Contact lens wearers: take lenses out first** ("Take lenses out before removing makeup") [M] **S23**.
2. Soak a cotton pad or washcloth in **warm** (not hot) water and hold it on closed eyes for about **30 seconds** [M] **S7**.
3. Gently press and slide **downward** along the lashes with the pad or fingertips. The little tubes slide off. Thrive: "slowly move in downward swiping motions" [M] **S7**.
4. Rinse the sink: "it leaves a mess in the sink… I'd rather have a mess in my sink than a mess all over my eyes" [M] **Y3**.
5. Then cleanse the face as normal.

**Warnings from users** [M]:
- Use warm, not cold: "sometimes i'm too lazy to use hot water so i'll use cold water and it pulls some of my eyelashes out" (@Madilyn-sh5pd, **Y21** comments).
- It doesn't always "noodle off" neatly: Dominique Sachse wiggled her lashes with dry fingers and got nothing; at the sink it took "face soap as well as a oil-free eye makeup remover" to get Tarte's tubing off [M] **Y6**.
- In a chemist's test, one product sold as tubing just turned to "mush" in water and smudged, with no tubes [M] **Y8**.

**Regular (washable) mascara**
- Cleansing oil or balm first, then a gentle cleanser. Dr Dray: "use an oil cleanser… the oil will just dissolve that makeup… and it'll slide right off" then follow with a gentle cleanser; ophthalmologists often suggest **baby shampoo** for the lid edge [M] **Y13**.
- Micellar water on a pad works too; ophthalmologist Dr Rupa Wong warns some micellar waters contain alcohol and that she once had "a horrible allergic dermatitis reaction" to one [M] **Y16**.
- Use the **ring finger** and little circles; finish with a cotton bud along the lid edge to catch bits that could fall into the eye [M] **Y16**, **Y13**.

**Waterproof mascara**
- Needs an oil or **bi-phase** remover. Hold a soaked pad on the closed eye for 10–20 seconds, then wipe down; never scrub [M] **Y14**; L'Oréal advice for its mascara, **S8**.
- Ophthalmologist: waterproof "usually requires a biphasic makeup remover and… a lot more [rubbing]… that can cause lash loss, it can cause wrinkles" [M] **Y14**.

**For everyone**
- **Take it off every night.** Dr Dray describes a published case of a woman who slept in mascara for about 25 years until bits of it were "embedded into the eyelid", causing painful bumps [M] **Y12**, **Y13**.
- Don't use face scrubs or acne washes (benzoyl peroxide, salicylic or glycolic acid) around the eyes [M] **Y13**.

### 3.4 Common beginner mistakes

| Mistake | Why it's a problem | Fix | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pumping the wand | Pushes air in, dries the tube, causes clumps | Twist and swirl | [M] **Y9**, **Y18**, **S7** |
| Too much on the brush | Clumps, spider lashes | Wipe on the tube neck | [M] **Y7**, **Y18** |
| Eye cream or oil on the lashes | Mascara slides, smudges, clumps | Keep creams below the eye; clean lashes | [M] **Y7** |
| Looking straight ahead into the mirror | Dots on the lid | Mirror low, look down | [M] **Y9**, **Y18** |
| Curling after mascara | Breaks coated lashes | Curl first (or heated curler after) | [M] **Y2** |
| Waiting too long between coats (tubing) | Clumps, chunks | Second coat while wet | [M] **S7**, **Y3** |
| Too many coats | Heavy, droops, "Tammy Faye" (far too much mascara; see glossary) | One or two coats | [M] **Y3**, **Y6** |
| Heavy lower lashes | Shadows, "Baby Jane" (over-made-up; see glossary), smudges | Little or none | [M] **Y2**, **Y3** |
| Cold water on tubing | Pulls lashes | Warm water and patience | [M] **Y21** comments |
| Scrubbing waterproof off | Lash loss, irritation | Oil/bi-phase, soak, press | [M] **Y14**, **Y13** |
| Keeping a tube for a year | Bacteria, dried clumpy formula | Replace every 2–4 months | [M] **S9** |
| Adding water or spit to a dry tube | Contamination | Throw it out | [M] **S9** (via **S5**) |
| Sharing mascara or using shop testers | Germs | Never share | [M] **S9** |

### 3.5 How long before you see anything

| Effect | When | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Darker, longer-looking, separated lashes | **Immediately**, with the first coat | [R] how all mascara works |
| How well it lasts (smudging, flaking) | Judge at **the end of a full day** | [M] Dominique tested each "at the end of the day", **Y6** |
| Easy removal | That night | [R] |
| Brand's "visibly healthier" bare lashes | Brand says **4–6 weeks** | [M] **S1**; [R] no evidence; a lash's whole growing phase is 4–10 weeks (**S13**), so any real change would take months, not weeks |
| How a tube ages | Some users see changes at **45 days to "a couple of months"** | [M] "until about the 45 day mark and it just seems to change. It clumps and transfers" (@leonnahofer9138); "after a couple of months it dries" (@unik428) |

---

## 4. Where it fits in a routine

### 4.1 The order of steps

**Morning (makeup)**
1. Cleanse.
2. Skincare: moisturiser; eye cream **under** the eye, not on the lashes.
3. Sunscreen.
4. Contact lenses in (**before** makeup) [M] **S23**.
5. Face makeup: foundation (skin-coloured liquid that evens out the skin) or tinted moisturiser (a lighter, moisturising version), then concealer (thicker cream to hide dark circles).
6. Eyes: lid primer (a thin base cream) or loose powder (hooded lids) → eyeshadow (colour on the lid; optional) → eyeliner (a line drawn along the lashes; optional; on the skin, not the waterline) → brows (filling in the eyebrows).
7. Curl lashes (optional).
8. **Mascara: the last eye step.** Luxe goes here.
9. Blush (colour on the cheeks), lips.

[R] Mascara usually goes last on the eyes so eyeshadow dust doesn't land on wet lashes and so you can clean the lid up after.

**Night**
1. Contact lenses out first [M] **S23**.
2. **Remove the mascara** (warm water for tubing; oil for regular/waterproof).
3. Cleanse the face.
4. Skincare.
5. **Lash serum, last, on a clean, dry upper lash line** (e.g. the Luxe Eyelash Serum, pack 4).

[M] A creator (Cassi, **Y20**) describes this exact split: "I use a conditioning treatment in the morning and I use a lash serum at night" (**Y20**).

### 4.2 What it goes with, and what not to mix

| Combination | Good or bad | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Lash curler before | Good | Lift, then lock it with mascara |
| Eyeshadow primer or powder on the lid | Good | Stops lid oil breaking mascara down [M] **Y9** |
| Lash serum at night | Good | Different jobs; never at the same time [R] |
| Brown liner instead of black | Good for mature eyes | Softer; "I tend to like a dark chocolate compared to a black" [M] **Y19** |
| Rich eye cream or eye oil on or near lashes before mascara | Bad | Breaks mascara down; smudging [M] **Y7** |
| Castor oil on lashes in the morning | Bad | Castor oil is for night only: "you don't want to do that right before you put on your makeup because it is kind of thick" [M] **Y5** |
| A conditioning lash treatment then mascara on top | Often bad | Makes regular mascara clumpy; "Usually your choices are just don't wear mascara or don't use your conditioning treatment" [M] **Y20**; that is the problem Luxe claims to solve |
| Tubing mascara over waterproof, or a "refresher" over tubing | Bad | MAC's refresher "is not meant to go over waterproof… and it's not meant to go over tubing" [M] **Y3** |
| Regular mascara over a tubing base for an event | Works for some | "For an event I will apply a normal mascara over and/or under it for more oomph" [M] (@lorainemcgillivray77, **Y9** comments) |
| Lash extensions | Only with an oil-free formula | Oil loosens glue [M] **Y10**; Luxe says "Lash Extension Friendly" |
| Waterproof liner in the waterline | Avoid | Blocks oil glands [M] **Y14** |
| Fibre mascara with contact lenses | Avoid | Fibres get under lenses [M] **Y2**; snippet in **S5** |
| Prescription eye drops | Watch out | Drops and tears can break tubing: "when the tubing mascaras broke up" (@bethirwin7445) [M] |

### 4.3 Example routines

**A. The 3-minute "look awake" routine (any age)**
1. Clean, dry lashes.
2. One coat of mascara on the upper lashes, root to tip.
3. Nothing on the lower lashes.
4. Night: warm water, then face wash.

**B. A woman over 60 with hooded, sensitive eyes and reading glasses**
1. Morning: moisturiser; eye cream only under the eye; let it absorb.
2. A tiny amount of eyeshadow primer (a thin base cream for the lid) or a dusting of loose powder over the whole lid, so the lid's oil doesn't break the mascara down.
3. Optional: a soft brown eyeliner pencil smudged close to the upper lashes (skin side, not the waterline, the wet inner rim).
4. Optional: curl gently (heated curler if the metal one pinches).
5. Magnifying mirror placed low; chin up, eyes looking down.
6. Mascara (tubing or water-based, fragrance-free): one coat on the upper lashes, wiggle at the root; tip of the wand for corners. Second coat straight away only if needed.
7. Skip the lower lashes, or one light dab at the outer third.
8. Cotton bud on hand for touch-ups.
9. Night: lenses out (if worn); warm-water soak 30 seconds; slide off; gentle cleanser; lash serum on a dry lash line if used.
10. Replace the mascara every 3 months (write the date on the tube).

Sources for B: **Y2**, **Y3**, **Y9**, **Y18**, **Y19**, **S9**, **S23** [M]; the combination is my reading [R].

**C. Contact-lens wearer**
1. Hands washed; lenses in **first**.
2. Water-based or tubing mascara; no fibres; tips and top lashes only.
3. Never liner inside the lash line ("Don't apply eyeliner to the inner edge of the lid") [M] **S23**.
4. Night: lenses out **first**, then remove mascara [M] **S23**.

**D. A wedding, a funeral, a swim**
- Waterproof for one day is fine: "for occasional use of course waterproof mascara and eyeliner are fine" [M] **Y14**. A makeup artist keeps waterproof "for brides or mother of the brides anyone who thinks they're going to get emotional" [M] **Y3**.
- Remove carefully with an oil remover that night.

**E. Dry-eye routine (from an ophthalmologist)**
- Warm compress on closed eyes for 10–15 minutes (a sock filled with rice, warmed 30 seconds) to open the oil glands; lid wipes (ready-soaked pads for cleaning the lid edge); preservative-free artificial tears (eye drops that copy natural tears). Add makeup back slowly, one product at a time [M] **Y15**.

---

## 5. Who it's for and who it isn't

### 5.1 By age

| Age | Typical lashes and lids | What helps | Fit with Luxe's pitch [R] |
|---|---|---|---|
| Under 20 | not found: no source in this pack covers teenagers' lashes or mascara needs | — | Not the target |
| 20s–30s | Longest, thickest lashes (7.98 mm average at 22–29) [M] **S12**; firm lids | Anything; volume formulas | Not the target |
| 40s | Lids start to hood; richer eye creams; more smudging [M] **Y7** | Smudge-resistant, tubing | Partly |
| 50s | Lashes ~20% shorter and thinner [M] **S12** [R sum]; tear film (the thin tear layer over the eye) less stable; menopause (periods stop, hormones drop) | Lightweight, tubing, easy removal, separation | **Main target** |
| 60s–70s+ | Hooded lids, crepey skin, poorer near sight, cataract surgery, dry eyes, eye drops; some give up mascara | Precision brush, magnifying mirror, gentle removal, maybe brown-black; less on lower lashes | **Main target**; but watery eyes and drops can defeat tubing |

### 5.2 By lash type

| Lashes | Need | Luxe fit [R] |
|---|---|---|
| Short, sparse, fine | Length and separation without weight | Pitched for this ("grips short, fine lashes") |
| Straight / downward | Curl first; lifting brush or formula | Brand claims "Straight lashes get lift"; untested |
| Light / blonde | Colour matters most; black may look harsh | Black only on the shop page (two reviews on Luxe's page mention a brown / black-brown, #37, #68) |
| Thick, long | Volume formulas | Not the target |
| Lash extensions | Oil-free | Says "Lash Extension Friendly" |
| Brittle | Gentle removal more than "conditioning" | Warm-water removal (if true) is the real help |

### 5.3 By eye type

| Eyes | Issue | What helps | Luxe fit [R] |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hooded** | Lashes touch the lid → transfer | Tubing or waterproof; primer; curl | Fits if it really is tubing |
| **Oily lids** | Oil breaks wax mascara | Tubing (water-soluble polymers resist oil) [M] **Y8** | Fits if tubing |
| **Watery** | Warm tears dissolve tubing | Waterproof or regular | **Possibly poor fit** |
| **Dry eye / eye drops** | Drops break tubing; waterproof clogs glands | Gentle, fragrance-free, removed nightly | Uncertain |
| **Sensitive / allergic** | Fragrance, preservatives, nickel, wax | Short, fragrance-free list you can check | **Can't be checked: no list** |
| **Contact lenses** | Flakes and fibres under lenses | Water-based, no fibres | Claims "safe for contact lens wearers"; untested |
| **Deep-set** (eyes that sit further back under the brow bone) | Hard to reach | Slim brush | Plausible |
| **After eye surgery** | Infection risk | Wait for the surgeon's OK; new tube | Same as any mascara |

### 5.4 By budget

| Budget | Options | Where Luxe sits [M] |
|---|---|---|
| Under $10 | Essence Lash Princess $4.97, Maybelline Sky High $10.82 (Amazon prices from asinsight, a site that estimates Amazon prices and sales; see 7.1) | — |
| $10–20 | L'Oréal Lash Paradise $13.99, Telescopic $11.97 | Luxe on Amazon UK £14.99 |
| $20–30 | Thrive $26, Tarte $28, Grande $25, Ilia ~$29, PrimeLash $22.40–28 | Luxe "buy 1 get 1" works out at about $20 a tube [R] |
| $30+ | Lancôme Lash Idôle $30 (asinsight) / $40–45 (Face Value), Dior $33–44 | Luxe $39.95 single on its own site |

Sources: **S5** (asinsight and brand pages, Jun–Aug 2026), **Y1**, **Y2**, **Y6**. Price-conscious viewers: "I don't want to spend over $25 for mascara especially when we need to replace them every 3 months" (@vanessawilen2417, 190 likes) [M].

### 5.5 What changes after 50 and 60, and why

1. **Fewer, shorter, finer lashes.** Measured: 20% shorter, thinner [M] **S12**. Why: lashes grow for only 4–10 weeks [M] **S13**; with age, follicles may spend longer resting (expert view, **Y2**). Result: mascara has less to grip; heavy formulas drag them down.
2. **Lids hood.** Extra skin folds over; lashes touch it [M] **Y7**. Result: transfer to the lid, "polka dots" on the brow bone (the ridge under the eyebrow) [M] **Y3**.
3. **Under-eye skin thins and creases.** Result: flakes and smudges sit in lines and look like dark circles. "tiny black confetti sits in your fine lines and dark circles" [M] **S2**; "Heavy, dry mascara emphasizes dark circles and every fine line" (makeup artist Caroline Hernandez, quoted in **Y2**) [M].
4. **More eye cream and richer skincare.** Result: more grease near lashes → smudging [M] **Y7**.
5. **Tear film changes; dry eye and eye drops.** Oil glands work less well; eyes sting and water [M] **Y2**, **Y15**. Meibomian gland problems are linked to preservatives, surfactants and essential oils near the eye in a 2026 review [M] **S17**.
6. **Allergies can start late.** "you can develop an allergic reaction to a product even if you've used that product one, ten or even a thousand times before" (Dr Rupa, **Y15**) [M]. Eyelids are a top site for cosmetic allergy [M] **S16**.
7. **Eyesight.** Hard to see lashes; cataract surgery; shaky hands [M] **Y5** comments.
8. **Less curl.** "As I got older I found that curling didn't work any longer" (@elenapizzarello9344) [M].
9. **Style shift.** Many want "less is more", softer black or brown-black: "a softer brown-black instead of a harsh jet black on fair or cool skin" [M] **Y2**. Some want to avoid looking "clownish" or "Baby Jane" [M] **S5**.

---

## 6. Every problem the viewer has, one by one

For each: what it is, why it happens, how it feels (real quotes), what people tried and why it failed, and how Luxe helps or doesn't. Quotes are from YouTube comments pulled with NexLev (**S5** raw file `data/mascara/nexlev-youtube-comments-2026-10-31.md`, plus pulls on 2026-10-31 noted as **Y9c**, **Y17c**, **Y21c**, now saved in `data/reviews/mascara/nexlev-youtube-comments-repull-2026-10-31.md`). Numbers like **#92** are reviews in the reviews half of this pack, which gives each one's source, date and stars or likes.

### Problem 1: "My lashes have disappeared" (short, sparse, light)
- **What:** Lashes too short or few to show; the eye looks bare and tired.
- **Why:** Lashes ~20% shorter and thinner after 50 [M] **S12**; finer hairs reflect less.
- **How it feels:** "I have blonde thin and straight lashes that are invisible without mascara" (@nedamm5677, 18 likes). "I didn't feel pulled together like I once did" (@elenapizzarello9344). Luxe's own copy plays on it: "This isn't another mascara that looks amazing on a 25-year-old model and invisible on you" [M] **S1**.
- **Tried and failed:** Volume mascaras that clump on sparse lashes; extensions (cost, damage; one commenter got them after mascara stopped working); false lashes; serums with side-effect fears (pack 4).
- **Luxe [R]:** Its pitch fits exactly (slim "precision" wand, "featherlight"). Any mascara darkens and lengthens instantly; whether Luxe's brush catches tiny corner lashes better than a $5–11 slim brush (Essence, Sky High) is **untested**. It will not regrow lashes.

### Problem 2: Smudging, transfer and "raccoon eyes"
- **What:** Black marks under the eye or on the lid by midday.
- **Why:** Lid oil, eye cream, tears, heat and hooded skin soften wax mascaras; lashes droop and touch the skin [M] **Y7**, **Y3**, **Y8**.
- **How it feels:** "Every single mascara I've tried over the last 30 years plus ends up transferring onto the foundation under my eyes or just creating a very dark shadow underneath …" (@teddyroon, age 66, 0 likes). "Raccoon eyes have been the bane of my existence because I have significant chronic dry eye and have to use eye drops throughout the day" (@donkiml5805). "I've been struggling with mascara ending up under my eyes for YEARS" (@danikafarnsworth5590).
- **Tried and failed:** Waterproof (hard removal; "even waterproof mascara would end up looking like raccoon eyes", @NIKKIRHODES1212); expensive brands ("YSL… it smudge!", @mikaelamichael7456); skipping lower lashes.
- **Luxe [R]:** If it is a true tubing formula, this is its strongest benefit: tubes "do not smudge from anything from oil, sweat, tears, or even eye cream" (**Y4**). But the page also says "Waterproof".

### Problem 3: Flakes in fine lines
- **What:** Dry crumbs falling under the eyes.
- **Why:** Wax-and-pigment coats dry stiff and crack; old tubes dry out.
- **How it feels:** "start to crumble from your eyes around lunch time" (@unik428). "Halfway through the day, flaking" (reviewer quoted in **Y1**).
- **Tried and failed:** Prestige volume mascaras; fresh tubes that go off after weeks.
- **Luxe [R]:** Claims "zero flaking"; tubing generally flakes less ("tubing mascara is incapable of flaking", makeup artist **Y3**; though one Thrive user said "after few weeks of using it, it would start to flake", @VictoriaBC610). Untested for Luxe.

### Problem 4: Hard removal that pulls lashes out
- **What:** Rubbing and scrubbing at night; lashes on the cotton pad.
- **Why:** Waterproof and long-wear formulas need oil and friction; rubbing breaks and pulls lashes [M] **Y14**, **S13**.
- **How it feels:** "If i have to scrub my eyes to get it off that is a deal breaker" (@dfollmer1428). "whenever I take off waterproof mascara it always rips out my lashes and takes so long" (@josiejohnson_28). "how easily they remove is just as important to me as how they perform" (@Keeper_of_the_Hearth). Luxe: "Every lash left on a cotton pad feels personal when you already have fewer to spare" [M] **S2**.
- **Tried and failed:** Waterproof + bi-phase remover; wipes (Dr Dray: wipes "require more rubbing" and cause "eyelash breakage", **Y13**).
- **Luxe [R]:** "Warm water dissolves it cleanly" is the centre of the pitch. If true, it helps. Remember tubing can still need patience and warm water: Dominique needed soap and remover to get Tarte's tubing off (**Y6**), and cold water can pull lashes (**Y21c**).

### Problem 5: Sensitive, stinging, itchy, watering eyes
- **What:** Eyes burn, water or itch, lids go red after mascara.
- **Why:** Allergy or irritation to fragrance, preservatives, wax, nickel, alcohol; dry eye; blocked oil glands [M] **Y15**, **Y12**, **S16**, **S17**.
- **How it feels:** "Essence looked great but caused an itching and burning every time I wore it" (@JoyWinters-t4h). "some black mascaras just make my eyes water and hurt" (@AudriannaB-World-Peace). Molly Burke, quoted by Dr Rupa: "mascaras, makeup removers, eye creams i burn my eyes make my eyes water" (**Y15**).
- **Tried and failed:** "Hypoallergenic" products (term has no legal meaning, **S10**); "sensitive skin" micellar water that still caused a reaction (**Y15**, **Y16**).
- **Luxe [R]:** Claims hypoallergenic, pH balanced, water-based. **But with no ingredient list, someone with a known allergy cannot check it.** Patch test and stop if it stings.

### Problem 6: Watery eyes and eye drops break the mascara
- **What:** Tears and drops make mascara run, even tubing.
- **Why:**
  - Tubing films are made of water-loving plastics: a chemist calls the main one "the water soluble polymer that makes tubing mascaras tube" [M] **Y8**. Warm water is exactly what brands tell you to use to take them off [M] **S7**.
  - Tears are warm water, and eye drops are watery too [R]. So a watery eye slowly does to the mascara what the warm cloth does at night.
  - Older eyes water more, because the tear film (the thin tear layer over the eye) becomes less stable: "mature eyes water more" [M] **Y2**; Dr Rupa, **Y15**.
  - Heat, humidity and sweat add more warm water [R].
- **How it feels:** "Tears are warm so they cause the mascara to smear, just as warm water does" (@JoyWinters-t4h). "Victoria Beckham's Vast Lash… holds up to the multiple medicated eye drops I have to use when the tubing mascaras broke up" (@bethirwin7445). "Tubing mascaras smudge and run down my face from May-December in the heat and humidity of Florida" (@Corrine919).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Tubing mascara: it broke up under eye drops, so one woman moved to another mascara: "Victoria Beckham's Vast Lash… holds up to the multiple medicated eye drops I have to use when the tubing mascaras broke up" (@bethirwin7445, 3 likes, **Y4** comments, review #254). Sky Tubes: "do not wear it when you think you might cry, ot runs everywhere, and starts coming off in cubes" (#183).
  - Waterproof: [R] it resists tears better, but even that failed in heat: "working in the salon in Georgia was like working in a saunafied sweat shop and even waterproof mascara would end up looking like raccoon eyes" (@NIKKIRHODES1212, review #265). And waterproof is harsh to take off: "found the irritation from removing it everyday caused issues with my rosacea at my eyes" (@lorainemcgillivray77, 2 likes, **Y9c**).
  - Skipping the lower lashes: "I've learned with using eye drops, It's better to not use mascara on the lower eyelashes or only in the outer corners" (@donkiml5805, review #257). It helps, but the top lashes still run.
- **Luxe [R]:** If it is warm-water tubing, this is its **weak spot**. One review on Luxe's own page says the opposite: "i have very watery eyes… it stays on perfectly no matter how much my eyes water" (#41); remember that 78 of the 80 Luxe reviews sit on the waterproof listing.

### Problem 7: Contact lenses
- **What:** Bits of mascara or fibres get under lenses; lenses cloud.
- **Why:** Mascara sticks to lenses: non-waterproof mascara left 0.9–7.1% coverage on silicone hydrogel lenses (a common kind of soft contact lens) after an hour's soak in a lab, depending on lens material [M] **S15**. Flakes or fibres that fall into the eye can also get caught under a lens (fibres: **Y2**) [M].
- **How it feels:** "I wore contacts for over 50 years" and preferred no mascara (Rebecca, 70, **Y5**). Luxe ad persona: "My eyes water at everything and I wear contacts, so most mascaras are a nightmare" [M] **S2** (brand copy, not a verified customer).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Fibre mascaras: the fibres shed into the eye (**Y2**), so lens wearers are told to avoid them (**S23**; snippet in **S5**).
  - Waterproof: a contact-lens shop's advice page says to avoid waterproof mascara with lenses (feelgoodcontacts.com, search snippet in `data/mascara/websearch-snippets-2026-10-31.md`), and it needs oily removers and rubbing (**Y14**).
  - A "tubing" mascara that wasn't: a long-time Thrive user switched to PrimeLash, which claimed warm-water removal but "required an oil based makeup remover to come off cleanly at night" (#82). She went back to Thrive.
  - A Luxe page review says it "doesnt flake off into my contacts" (#68, brand's own site).
- **Luxe [R]:** Claims "safe for contact lens wearers"; no lens test named. Rivals publish one (Thrive: "Dermatologist + ophthalmologist tested… safe for contact lens wearers", **S7**). Lens rules apply regardless (3.3, 4.3C).

### Problem 8: Straight lashes that won't curl or hold a curl; curlers that pinch
- **What:** Lashes point straight out or down; curl drops in an hour.
- **Why:** Natural shape; heavy wet formulas weigh lashes down; regular mascara relaxes curl.
- **How it feels:** "They are short and cannot hold a curl at all" (@michelleyork7647). "There are plenty of us out here… who keep getting pinched by the curlers or who are unable to get the curlers in the right position to work. I'm looking for a good self curling mascara" (@user-zk8ed4kd2b). "Anything not waterproof will wilt your freshly curled lashes quicker than you can say 'Flop'" (@DSmith-ix1xf).
- **Tried and failed:** Cheap curlers ("cheap ones have the potential of pulling your eyelashes out", **Y9**); waterproof just for curl.
- **Luxe [R]:** Claims "lifts without weighing down"; brand's own line "Curlers lose their grip after 20 minutes" [M] **S1**. Untested. Tubing films can hold curl (Thrive says "lifts and curls"), but evidence for Luxe is none.

### Problem 9: Clumps and "spider legs"
- **What:** Lashes stick together in thick spikes.
- **Why:** Too much on the brush; thick formula; dried tube; second coat on a dry first coat.
- **Why (more):** tubing formulas set fast, so a second coat on a dry first coat sticks lashes together: "If it gets dry and then you add a coat it is more prone to clumping" (@CarolZone8A, 12 likes, #252). A tube that has started to dry out gives thicker, lumpier formula (#109; see Problem 11).
- **How it feels:** "I bought the Hourglass on your recommendation and it is sooo clumpy!! …" (@Rachierooh, 2 likes). "I cannot apply 2 coats due to clumping but even one coat clumps" (PrimeLash reviewer, snippet, **S5**).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Waiting between eyes, or not: "I wait a minute then go to the other eye and I get clumps that I have to comb out. Which ends up removing a lot of the product. I've tried less time and more time between eyes and get the same results" (#115, Thrive, age 55–64).
  - Wiping the brush first: "Thrive left globs of mascara on my lashes in spite of skimming brush prior to application" (@Marlenabkk, 4 likes, #239).
  - Paying more: "Thought something that cost more than double my old standby did must be totally awesome… the brush leaves clumps" (#116, Thrive, 65+).
  - A new tube: "The first application from the brand new tube was wonderful… But after the initial use, the mascara goes on clumpy" (#109).
- **Luxe [R]:** Claims "Zero clumps"; the one independent short says "Absolutely zero clumps" after ¾ of a coat (**Y20**). Untested beyond that.

### Problem 10: Can't see to apply; hooded lids get covered; shaky hands
- **What:** Mascara on the lid, the brow bone, the nose.
- **Why:**
  - Near sight fades with age, so close-up work like lashes gets blurry without glasses, and glasses get in the way of the wand [R]. Cataracts make it worse (@adriennekushner1968, below).
  - Hooding: the extra lid skin hangs down so "things are touching now" (Nina Ubhi, **Y7**), and the lashes may sit hidden under the fold.
  - Shaky or stiff hands (tremor, arthritis) make a thick, bushy brush hard to control (#35, #57, #66) [M].
- **How it feels:** "it made my hooded eyes look smaller and I really couldn't see my eyelashes anymore to apply it and got more on my eyelids than lashes" (@Kim-ek7xy, almost 70). "I even try to put tape on my eyelids before applying mascara" (@HurshRoad, **Y9c**). "my lashline tucks up behind that immobile skin flap 'hood' lol" (@errrkt, **Y9c**).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Tape on the lid: "I even try to put tape on my eyelids before applying mascara" (@HurshRoad, 0 likes, **Y9c**); in the same comment she says "I struggle so much with mascara".
  - Carrying on without seeing: "I really couldn't see my eyelashes anymore to apply it and got more on my eyelids than lashes" (@Kim-ek7xy, 30 likes, #273), so she stopped wearing mascara.
  - Big, bushy brushes: "it doesnt get mess on my eyelids like those big bushy brushes do" (#66, Luxe page).
  - What worked for one: "When I had my cataract surgery, I bought a 10X mirror so I could see up close. It is a game changer for me" (@adriennekushner1968, 42 likes, #276).
- **Fixes:** Low magnifying mirror, look down (**Y9**, **Y18**); primer (**Y9**); slim brush (**Y1**: Rare Beauty's tip for lower lashes).
- **Luxe [R]:** A slim wand helps; the technique matters more than the brand.

### Problem 11: Tubes that dry out or "go off"; the cost of replacing
- **What:** A tube changes after 6–8 weeks: clumps, flakes, transfer.
- **Why:**
  - Pumping the wand pushes air into the tube and "dries out the mascara" (**Y18**) [M].
  - The water or solvent slowly evaporates each time the tube is opened, so the formula thickens [R].
  - Germs from the eye ride back into the tube on the wand; the FDA says to throw mascara out 2–4 months after buying it (**S9**) [M].
- **How it feels:** "until about the 45 day mark and it just seems to change" (@leonnahofer9138). "I dread to think how much money I have spent trying to find the one" (@lornacartwright1341).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Taking great care: "I am very careful to close the tube completely… It just didn't last longer than one use" (#109).
  - Switching to a pricier "long-wear" tubing: it still went off: "Tartalette is good in the beginning, but after a couple of months it dries or something" (@unik428, 11 likes, #240); Thrive "after few weeks of using it, it would start to flake" (@VictoriaBC610, 2 likes, #255).
  - Buying cheap so it hurts less: "if it dries up in 2 months, at least I didn't spend over $20" (@vanessawilen2417, 190 likes, #222).
  - What helped one: "I don't pump air into the tube and I make sure I seal it up tight… The tube lasts quite some time" (@MmeO, age 67, 11 likes, #237).
- **Luxe [R]:** Brand page gives no shelf life. At $39.95 single, a 3-monthly replacement is pricey; the 2-for-1 or Amazon price makes more sense.

### Problem 12: Too harsh; wants brown
- **What:** Jet black looks stark on fair, older skin.
- **Why:**
  - With age, skin often gets paler and thinner, hair and brows go grey, and lashes get fewer and finer. A solid black line then stands out much more against everything around it [R].
  - Makeup artists quoted in **Y2** recommend "a softer brown-black instead of a harsh jet black on fair or cool skin" [M].
  - Some lashes do start to grey: "my lashes are graying" (#68, Luxe page), though the review study says lashes grey "only at a very advanced age" (**S13**).
- **How it feels:** "My problem is that I want brown mascara, not black. Black simply does not work for me and that is often the only choice there is." (@mlaiuppa, 1 like). "If I use mascara on all my lashes, I get a Bette Davis as Baby Jane look" (@phyllistallent4149, 57 likes; "Baby Jane" = over-made-up, see glossary). "I do not wish to appear clownish" (@lindap.p.1337, age 77, 30 likes).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - "Brown-black" shades: one woman found them still harsh: the Tarte brown is "a very nice true brown, not the black brown that other brands have offered for years that are harsh" (@susanmcf1, #259).
  - A brown she loved was discontinued: "according to a Blinc CSR the Medium Brown shade has been discontinued… I really miss it!" (#144).
  - Softer colours that went too far: "The color (Elena) is so subtle, you don't see the tint" (#116); "The Mulberry color makes me look like I have Pink-Eye" (#108).
  - Using less: mascara "only on the outer 1/4 to 1/3 of my lashes" (@phyllistallent4149).
- **Fixes:** Brown-black shades (Thrive's brown-black is "the one older reviewers reach for", **Y2**); Tarte tubing in brown (**Y4**); navy (**Y18**).
- **Luxe [R]:** **Black only** on the shop page. Two reviews on Luxe's own page mention a brown / black-brown shade (#37 "i got the brown… not harsh like black can be on older skin"; #68 "this black-brown color is perfect"); the shop lists Black only. So for viewers who want brown, it is not clear Luxe sells one.

### Problem 13: Lower lashes smudge or "cast shadows"
- **What:** Mascara on the bottom row prints onto the skin under the eye, or makes the eye look tired and sunken.
- **Why:**
  - The lower lashes sit right against the under-eye skin, which is wet with tears, often coated in eye cream, and creased with age. Every blink or smile presses lashes onto it [R].
  - Dark pigment there reads as a shadow: "Loading the bottom row drops dark pigment right against the under eye, where it deepens shadows" (**Y2**) [M]; film and TV makeup artists avoid it because it can "cast shadows and can end up making you look more hollowed out" (**Y3**) [M].
  - The lower lashes curve more downward with age (**S13**), so they touch the skin more [M].
- **How it feels:** "This is the mascara for women who gave up on lower lashes years ago" (**Y1**). "Loading the bottom row drops dark pigment right against the under eye, where it deepens shadows" (**Y2**).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Skipping them completely: "I personally feel not touching them at all gives the eye and unfinished look" (@DianehiltzCanada, 5 likes, **Y9c**).
  - Eye drops all day: "It's better to not use mascara on the lower eyelashes or only in the outer corners" (@donkiml5805, #257).
  - Not everyone has the problem: "I am in my 60s… I have always applied mascara to the lower lash line with the mascara wand and I have never had issues" (@susanstukins2990, 19 likes, **Y17c**).
- **Fixes:** Skip; dab; vertical wand; tissue; a tubing formula on the bottom only (comments in **Y17c**).
- **Luxe [R]:** No specific claim beyond "tiny ones in the corners".

### Problem 14: Wants lash health without serum side effects
- **What:** Fear of lash serums (prostaglandins can darken the iris, the coloured ring of the eye) but wanting "healthier" lashes.
- **Why:**
  - Lashes really do get shorter and thinner with age (**S12**), and rough removal breaks them (**S13**, **Y14**) [M].
  - The serums that work best contain prostaglandins, whose possible side effects include a browner iris that the Latisse label calls "likely to be permanent" (pack 4, its S7) and redness and stinging (**Y15**) [M]. So people look for a gentler "middle path" [R].
- **How it feels:** "I have light blue eyes and I'm scared to ruin my eye color just for longer lashes" [M] **S5** (pack 4 data). "Should we use a lash serum such as Sweed or Grande Lash?" (@carolgarrett5650).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - A "conditioning" mascara: "Seeing that it functioned as a conditioning product I placed an order… Regrettably used only once and now sits in a throw away box" (#104, Thrive, age 55–64).
  - Beliefs that a mascara grows lashes: "My fav is Rimmel it literally grows your lashes" (@margieshelnutt9006, age 68, 73 likes, #235). No study backs mascara growth (2.3B) [R].
  - Castor oil at night (**Y5**, **Y1**): many use it; no good evidence it grows lashes [R].
  - A serum with a mascara on top worked for one: "I've also been using Lash Boost from Rodan & Fields for 8 months. The combination has totally changed my lashes" (#165). (A serum, not the mascara, is the likely cause [R].)
- **Luxe [R]:** "Conditioning peptide" sounds like a gentle middle path. In truth a peptide in mascara has no good evidence for growth (2.3B). Some mascaras do contain a prostaglandin (isopropyl cloprostenate) per an ophthalmologist (**Y15**); **Luxe's list is unknown**, so photograph the box.

### Problem 15: "I've given up on mascara"
- **What:** Women who stopped wearing mascara altogether, often after years of trouble.
- **Why:** it is every problem above added together. The reasons given in the reviews [M]:
  - The mess and the removal: "I am sick of the mess!" (#269); "I don't dread removing markup!!" (#271).
  - Stinging and watering: "My eyes water frequently and itch a lot. Consequently, I couldn't wear mascara" (#92); "I would end up with irritated eyelids. I had quit even bothering …" (#96).
  - Can't see to put it on, hooded lids: "I really couldn't see my eyelashes anymore to apply it" (#273).
  - Lashes too sparse for it to show: "I guess my lashes are just too old" (#83).
  - Infection: "I got an eyelash infection - so no false lashes or mascara allowed" (#277).
- **How it feels:** "I am 69 and just recently decided to ditch masacra. Its a bit scary but I am sick of the mess!" (@loftonrudolph7586, 57 likes). "Love not using mascara any more… I don't dread removing markup!!" (@rebeccaholley1544, 44 likes). "I don't like how it feels & it's messy to remove… I turned 76" (@patwagner9308). Rebecca, 70: "mascaras are basically putting tar on your lashes" (**Y5**).
- **What they tried and why it failed:**
  - Changing brands for years: "I had used the same mascara for probably 40 years and it had started irritating my eyes to the point that I usually just didn't bother to put it on… I have tried others through the years but always went back with my old standby" (#85, Thrive, 65+).
  - Curling harder and switching brands: "As I got older I found that curling didn't work any longer and no matter what brand of mascara I used, nothing helped my lashes" (@elenapizzarello9344, 34 likes, #226); she moved to lash extensions.
  - Two coats and a curler: "After curling, and with two coats, my lashes were still flat and stuck together" (#83).
- **The other side:** "I will never give up my mascara.EVER!" (@robiness5344, 125 likes, 29 replies). "At 64 years old, it makes me feel pretty." (@jamiebrennan5779, age 64, 76 likes).
- **Luxe [R]:** Tubing is often the reason women come back: "For a lot of women past 50, this is the reason they start wearing mascara again after quitting in frustration" (**Y1**); "I never wore mascara because it always smeared. But I recently starred wearing tubing mascara and it makes a huge difference" (@ItzaMuMu). Luxe's whole pitch aims at this group.

### 7.1 The main alternatives, explained simply

Prices are as found on the date and source given; they move. Amazon ratings come from asinsight.com reports, which estimate Amazon sales (month shown) [M] **S5**.

| Product | Type | What it is, plainly | Price (source, date) | What's different from Luxe [R] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Luxe Conditioning Peptide Mascara** | Says "lash-casting" (reads as tubing) + peptide | Over-50, sensitive-eye pitch; black only; no ingredient list | $39.95 brand site; £14.99 Amazon UK; US Amazon not found (**S1**, **S4**, 2026-10-31) | — |
| **Thrive Causemetics Liquid Lash Extensions** | Tubing | The best-known tubing mascara; brown-black option; ophthalmologist-tested; full list published | $26 (**S7**, 2026-10-31); Amazon 4.4★ (4.1K–4.6K, Jul–Aug 2026) | Published list and lens testing; cheaper single tube; brown-black; "No. 1" for over-50s in two big lists (**Y1**, **Y2**) |
| **Tarte Tartelette Tubing** (and XL "with Peptide") | Tubing (XL adds peptide) | Popular tubing; also in brown (**Y4**) | $28 Amazon, 4.4★ (4K), est. 100K units/month (asinsight Jul 2026) | Same idea plus a peptide; brown option; big seller |
| **Clinique Lash Power** | Tubing | "true tubing… Fragrance-free. Ophthalmologist tested" | $28–35 (**Y1**) or about $25 (**Y2**); sources differ | Fragrance-free and lens-friendly by name; less volume |

| **Essence Lash Princess** | Regular, fibre brush | The cheapest cult mascara | $4.97, 4.3★ (401.1K) | One-eighth of the price; Dominique: "wore exceptionally well… no flaking, no smudging" (**Y6**); some get itching (@JoyWinters-t4h) |
| **L'Oréal Lash Paradise (washable)** | Regular | Soft wavy brush; "Ophthalmologist-tested… sensitive eyes and contact lens wearers" (**S8**) | $13.99 (**S8**) | Cheap, published list; regular formula |

### 7.2 When to pick Luxe, and when something else [R]

**Luxe could make sense if:**
- You want a tubing-style, warm-water-removal mascara **and** like the idea of "conditioning" in one tube.
- You buy it at the Amazon price or the 2-for-1 deal, not $39.95 for one.
- You have short, fine lashes, hooded or oily lids, and no known ingredient allergies.

**Pick something else if:**
- **You have known allergies or very reactive eyes** → choose a product with a published list you (or your doctor) can check: Clinique Lash Power, Thrive, Lash Paradise.
- **You want brown or brown-black** → Thrive (brown-black), Tarte (brown), Blinc (dark brown), Grande (brown).
- **Your eyes water a lot or you use drops all day** → tubing may break down; a waterproof for occasional days, or a regular formula, may last better.
- **You're on a tight budget** → Essence ($4.97) and Sky High ($10.82) performed as well as a $44 Dior in a week-long test: "It's really hard to tell the difference between the $4 one and the $44 one" (**Y6**) [M].
- **You want proof of eye-doctor testing** → Thrive and L'Oréal state it on their own pages (**S7**, **S8**).
- **You want real lash growth** → that's a serum's job, not a mascara's (pack 4).

## 8. Questions viewers ask, with plain answers

Each question comes from a real comment or search, shown in brackets. Answers are plain summaries of the sources; [R] marks my reading.

1. **"What is tubing mascara?"** (the top search theme: "tubing mascara what is", **S19**) — A mascara that dries into tiny flexible sleeves around each lash. It doesn't smudge with oil and slides off with warm water. **Y3**, **S7**.
2. **"Is Luxe mascara a tubing mascara?"** (search "luxe mascara waterproof conditioning peptide mascara", **S4**) — The brand describes "a light, flexible cast… a soft, flexible tube", which sounds like tubing, but it also says "Waterproof". There's no ingredient list to confirm. [R] A warm-water rinse would show it: a true tubing formula slides off in little tubes.
3. **"What's in it?"** (Scam Advice video: "check the full ingredient list", **S5**) — Not published online. Check the box.
4. **"Does a peptide mascara grow lashes?"** (@carolgarrett5650 on serums; Luxe FAQ "Does it actually help my lashes grow, or is that just marketing?") — No good evidence. Growth claims belong to serums and drugs. **S18**, **S5**.
5. **"Will it sting my eyes?"** (Luxe FAQ; @AudriannaB-World-Peace) — It's sold as hypoallergenic and pH balanced, but "hypoallergenic" has no legal meaning (**S10**). Patch test; stop if it stings.
6. **"Can I wear it with contact lenses?"** (Luxe FAQ; Rebecca, **Y5**) — The brand says yes; no test is published. Put lenses in before, take them out before removing it, avoid fibres. **S23**.
7. **"How do I get tubing mascara off?"** (@Madilyn-sh5pd) — Warm water, a 30-second soak, then gentle downward strokes. Not cold water. **S7**, **Y21c**.
8. **"Why won't it come off in little tubes like on TikTok?"** (Dominique, **Y6**) — Dry fingers aren't enough; it needs warm water. Some products sold as tubing don't really tube (**Y8**).
9. **"Why does my mascara smudge under my eyes?"** (@danikafarnsworth5590) — Oil from lids and eye cream, tears, hooded lids and drooping lashes. Prime, keep creams off lashes, try tubing. **Y7**, **Y9**.
10. **"Is waterproof mascara bad for older eyes?"** (@carolegriffith7198: "Waterproof seems to be a problem") — Not dangerous for occasional use, but harder to remove (rubbing, lash loss) and can clog oil glands with daily use. **Y14**.
11. **"Do waterproof mascaras contain forever chemicals?"** (Face Value and Glow Audit videos) — A 2021 Notre Dame study found high fluorine (a sign of PFAS) in 47% of mascaras tested and in many waterproof ones (82% is quoted in press), mostly not on the label. It measured fluorine, not harm. **S20**.
12. **"Should I put mascara on my bottom lashes?"** (@DianehiltzCanada) — Optional. Many artists use little or none after 40 because it casts shadows. If you do, dab lightly. **Y3**, **Y2**.
13. **"How do I stop getting mascara on my eyelids?"** (@HurshRoad) — Mirror below you, look down, prime the lid, wipe extra off the wand. **Y9**, **Y18**.
14. **"Should I curl before or after?"** (curler comments, **Y9c**) — Before, with a metal curler; after, only with a heated one. **Y2**, **Y18**.
15. **"Can a curler make smudging worse?"** (@SwampSagacity1014, **Y9c**: curlers "can make the mascara smudging problem worse by curling them backward to touch your hooded lid. They can also make your eyelashes look shorter") — On a hooded lid it can: a very strong curl pushes the lashes up into the fold of skin, where they print mascara [R, from her comment]. Curl gently and prime the lid (**Y9**). Cheap curlers can also pull lashes out (**Y9**), though a study found curler use didn't measurably change lash structure (**S14**).
16. **"Is there a self-curling mascara?"** (@user-zk8ed4kd2b) — Curling formulas and curved brushes help (Lash Idôle, Telescopic Lift, Dior Overcurl, Charlotte Tilbury), but none replaces a curler on very straight lashes. **Y1**, **Y2**, **Y7**.
17. **"How often should I replace mascara?"** (@vanessawilen2417: "we need to replace them every 3 months") — FDA: 2–4 months after purchase. Dr Dray: "about three months". **S9**, **Y12**.
18. **"Why is my new mascara clumpy?"** (@Rachierooh) — Too much on the wand, residue on lashes, waiting too long between coats, or pumping. **Y7**, **Y9**.
19. **"Is there a brown version?"** (@mlaiuppa) — The Luxe shop lists Black only, though two reviews on Luxe's own page mention a brown / black-brown (#37, #68). Brown tubing: Tarte, Blinc; brown-black: Thrive. **S1**, **Y4**, **Y10**, **Y2**.
20. **"Is it cruelty-free?"** (@theasullivan4227, 17 likes) — The brand says so; no Leaping Bunny or PETA (an animal-rights group that keeps a cruelty-free list) listing was found, so say "the brand says". **S5**.
21. **"Does it work for hooded eyes?"** (@Kim-ek7xy, almost 70: "it made my hooded eyes look smaller and I really couldn't see my eyelashes anymore to apply it", 30 likes, **Y5** comments) — Tubing or smudge-resistant formulas help most, plus primer and technique. Untested for Luxe. **Y7**, **Y9**.
22. **"I use eye drops all day. Which mascara?"** (@donkiml5805; @bethirwin7445) — Drops can break tubing. Gentle, fragrance-free, removed nightly; talk to your eye doctor. **Y15**.
23. **"Can I use it with lash extensions?"** (Amazon title "Lash Extension Friendly") — The brand says so; oil-free formulas are the type that don't loosen glue. **Y10**.
24. **"Can I use a lash serum with it?"** (@carolgarrett5650) — Yes, at different times: mascara by day, serum at night on a clean lash line. **Y20**, pack 4.
25. **"Is drugstore as good as expensive?"** (@mireillefinch9276: "It's unlikely I'd ever spend $44 on mascara") — Often yes. Chemist: big brands "trickle down a lot of the same technology… to the more affordable options" (**Y8**); Dominique couldn't tell $4 from $44 (**Y6**).
26. **"Is the 'conditioning' real or marketing?"** (Luxe FAQ wording) — The softening oils and panthenol may make lashes feel softer, like hair conditioner. "Heals" and "growth" are not supported. **S6**, **S18**.
27. **"Why do I have to throw it out if it's still full?"** [R from **S9**] — Germs grow in the tube; in rare cases eye infections from cosmetics have caused blindness. **S9**.
28. **"Can I put castor oil on my lashes instead?"** (Rebecca, **Y5**; Face Value suggests it at night, **Y1**) — Many do at night. There's no good evidence it grows lashes; keep it out of the eye and off lashes before mascara. [R]
29. **"Does mascara make my lashes fall out?"** (@josiejohnson_28) — Wearing it isn't the main problem; frequent use is linked to more lash-surface cracking, and rough removal pulls lashes out. **S14**, **S13**.
30. **"What does 'ophthalmologist-tested' mean?"** — Very little on its own: "could simply mean as they send me a product in the mail, give me a survey" (Dr Rupa, **Y15**).
31. **"Is it waterproof?"** (Amazon CA snippet "water resistance"; Luxe page) — The brand says both "Waterproof" and "comes off with warm water". [R] It can't really be both.
32. **"How long does one tube last?"** — Size not found; replace every 2–4 months anyway (**S9**).
33. **"Can I wear mascara with deep-set eyes?"** (@meowwaffles6040, **Y9c**: "I thought I just couldn't wear mascara because of how deep set they are") — Yes. The tips that helped her are the hooded-eye ones: mirror below you, look down, wiggle the wand at the roots, and use the tip of the wand for corners (**Y9**). A slim brush reaches more easily (5.3).

---

## 9. Myths and truths

| Myth | Truth | Source |
|---|---|---|
| "A peptide mascara grows your lashes." | No mascara study shows growth. The nearest evidence is a serum trial with no comparison group. | **S18**, **S5** |
| "Hypoallergenic means it can't irritate." | In the US the word has no legal definition. | **S10** |
| "Ophthalmologist-tested means an eye doctor approved it." | It can mean as little as a mailed survey. | **Y15** |
| "Waterproof is best for older eyes." | It holds curl and survives tears, but removal is harsh and daily use can clog oil glands. Fine occasionally. | **Y14**, **Y3** |
| "Tubing never smudges." | Warm tears, eye drops, sweat and humidity can break it down. | comments (@Corrine919, @JoyWinters-t4h, @bethirwin7445); **Y3** |
| "Tubing comes off with a finger swipe." | It needs warm water and a soak; cold water can pull lashes. | **S7**, **Y6**, **Y21c** |
| "All 'tubing' mascaras are true tubing." | A chemist found one that just turned to "mush". | **Y8** |
| "Pump the wand to get more product." | Pumping dries it out and causes clumps. | **S7**, **Y9**, **Y18** |
| "Expensive mascara is better." | A $4.97 and a $10.82 mascara matched a $44 one in a week's test. | **Y6**, **Y8** |
| "Lash curlers ruin lashes." | A study found no significant change in lash structure from curler use; mascara frequency mattered more. Clean the curler; avoid if allergic to nickel. | **S14**, **Y12** |
| "You can add water to revive a dried tube." | It invites germs. Throw it out. | **S9** |
| "Mascara lasts until it's empty." | Replace 2–4 months after purchase. | **S9** |
| "Lashes go grey like hair." | Lashes grey only at a very advanced age and rarely go white. | **S13** |
| "More coats = more volume." | Many tubing formulas don't build; extra coats make chunks. | **Y3** |
| "Lower-lash mascara makes eyes bigger at any age." | Over 40 it often casts shadows and smudges. | **Y3**, **Y2** |
| "Natural ingredients can't cause allergy." | Wax and plant extracts can. | **Y15** |
| "Fibre mascara is a safe way to get length." | Fibres can fall into the eye and scratch the cornea. | **Y2** |
| "Use a pin to separate lashes." | Never: risk of stabbing the eye. Use a comb or spoolie. | **Y12**, **Y18** |

## 10. The words viewers use

## 11. Safety, and what you can and can't say on camera (short)

**Safety** [M]
- Patch-test new eye makeup; stop at stinging, itching, redness or swelling; see a doctor if it doesn't settle (**S9**, **Y15**).
- Don't use eye makeup during an eye infection; throw out what you were using (**S9**).
- After eye surgery, wait for the surgeon's OK [R].
- Replace 2–4 months after opening; never add water or saliva; never share; no shop testers (**S9**).
- Never apply in a moving car (**S9**).
- No pins near the eye (**Y12**).
- Contact lenses: in before makeup, out before removal (**S23**).
- Remove it every night (**Y12**, **Y13**).
- **Luxe's ingredient list is not published**: anyone with a known allergy, or worried about hidden prostaglandins (e.g. isopropyl cloprostenate, **Y15**), should read the box first.

**Can't say on camera** (drug-type claims under FDA rules, **S11**)
- "heals your lashes", "grows lashes", "fast growth", "regrows", "stops lash loss", "restores your 20-year-old lashes".
- Don't present the brand's "clinical" numbers (100%, 98%, 97%, "2,844 women") or "dermatologist-approved" as fact; no study was found.
- Don't say "hypoallergenic" or "safe for contacts" as a promise.

**Can say**
- "looks longer / fuller / darker", "separates", "didn't smudge on me after X hours", "came off with warm water in my test", "the brand says it contains conditioning peptides".

# Part 2: The reviews

**01 Mascara: the reviews**

Product 1: **Luxe Conditioning Peptide Mascara** (Amazon B0GSXJCZ93; sold on luxe.com, which is the same shop as luxecosmetics.us, and on Luxe's UK, Australian and other country shops). This file is the "reviews" half of pack 1. The explainer half is `packs/01-mascara-pack.md`.

Everything here was pulled on **2026-10-31**. Raw pulls are in `studies/luxe-cosmetics/data/reviews/mascara/`. The Amazon search-snippet figures have **unknown capture dates** (the search engine does not say when it read the page).

**Finishing pass, 2026-10-31.** A second helper checked this file (`checks/packs/01-mascara-pack-check.md`). Its fixes are applied here: theme P6 re-counted, missing YouTube dates and likes filled from a NexLev re-pull, cut quotes marked with "…", more words explained, Luxe's UK shop-wide review feed searched, and Amazon review text from search snippets added. Total is now **287** (#1–#287).

**Labels:** **[M]** = measured (copied from a page or feed, or counted by me from pulled data). **[R]** = my reading. "not found" = I looked and found nothing.

**Words used below (for a reader who has never worn makeup):**

| Word | What it means |
|---|---|
| Mascara | A black or coloured liquid brushed onto the eyelashes to make them look longer, thicker and darker. |
| Wand | The little brush inside the mascara tube. |
| Coat | One pass of the wand over the lashes. "Two coats" means brushing it on twice. |
| Clumps | Lashes sticking together in thick spiky bunches instead of looking like separate hairs. |
| Flaking | Little dry bits of mascara falling off the lashes onto the cheeks during the day. |
| Smudging / transfer / "raccoon eyes" / "panda eyes" | Mascara rubbing off onto the skin under or above the eye, leaving grey or black marks, like a raccoon's mask. |
| Tubing mascara | A type of mascara that wraps each lash in a thin tube of film. It does not smudge, and it slides off in little tubes when you wet the lashes with warm water. Thrive, Tarte, Blinc, Maybelline Sky Tubes and e.l.f. Lash Xtndr are tubing mascaras. Luxe's own advert describes its mascara the same way ("a light, flexible cast"). |
| Waterproof mascara | Mascara that resists water. It needs an oil or a special remover to take it off. |
| Lash line | The edge of the eyelid where the lashes grow. |
| Lash curler | A small clamp that bends the lashes upward before mascara goes on. |
| Hooded eyes | When the skin above the eye folds down over the eyelid, often more with age, so there is little visible lid. |
| Primer | A first coat put on before mascara to help it grip or add length. |
| Holy grail | Beauty slang for "the best one I have ever found". |
| Drugstore | Cheap brands sold in supermarkets and chemists (pharmacies), e.g. Maybelline, Essence, e.l.f. The opposite is "high end" or "prestige" (department-store brands). |
| Review box (review widget) | The block of customer reviews at the bottom of a shop's product page. Judge.me and Yotpo are two companies that run these boxes for online shops; the reviews here were read from the same public data the box loads. |
| Verified buyer | A mark the review box adds when it can match the review to a real order in that shop. "Not marked verified" means the box did not add that mark. |
| Incentivised review | The reviewer got something (a free product, a discount, points) in return for the review. Some review boxes mark these. |
| Lash extensions | Single fake lashes glued one by one onto your own lashes by a beautician. They last a few weeks. |
| Lash lift | A home or salon treatment that curls your own lashes with a lotion, like a perm for lashes. Lasts weeks. Luxe's best-known product is a lash-lift kit. |
| Lash tint | A dye that darkens your own lashes for a few weeks, so they show without mascara. |
| Eye makeup remover | A liquid or oil made to dissolve eye makeup. "Oil-based" removers and cleansing oils or balms dissolve waterproof mascara. Micellar water is a water-like remover used on a cotton pad. |
| Foundation | A skin-coloured cream or liquid spread over the face to even out the skin's colour. |
| Primer (eyeshadow primer) | A thin base put on the eyelid first so makeup on top stays put and does not crease or smudge. |
| Eyeliner | A pencil or liquid used to draw a line along the lash line. |
| Menopause / hot flashes | The time, usually around 45–55, when a woman's periods stop. Hot flashes (hot flushes) are sudden waves of heat and sweating that often come with it. |

**Words inside the quotes** (things reviewers mention without explaining):

| Word in a quote | What it means |
|---|---|
| LLE | Short for Thrive's "Liquid Lash Extensions" mascara (#82 and others). |
| Brynn, Crystal, Elena, Mulberry | Names of Thrive's mascara colours (Brynn = black, Crystal = brown-black; Elena and Mulberry are softer or coloured shades). |
| Carmine | A red dye made from crushed insects. Some people react to it (#176). |
| EWG | Environmental Working Group, a US group that rates cosmetic ingredients on its website. |
| Spoolie | A clean, dry mascara-style brush used to comb lashes or brows. |
| Upper blepharoplasty | Eyelid surgery that removes extra skin from the upper lid (#84). |
| SM platform | Social media (Instagram, TikTok and so on). |
| Wirecutter | A product-review website run by The New York Times (#111). |
| Ipsy bag | A monthly subscription bag of beauty samples (#166). |
| "Ulta's raincoat" | A clear waterproof top coat painted over mascara, sold by the US shop Ulta (#166). |
| CSR | Customer service representative (#144). |
| GF | Girlfriend (here, a female friend) (#94). |
| Pink-Eye | Conjunctivitis, an eye infection that makes the eye look red or pink (#108). |
| Formaldehyde | A preservative chemical some people are allergic to (#100). |
| Volumizer | A mascara made to make lashes look thicker. |
| Hypothyroidism | An underactive thyroid gland; it can thin the hair, brows and lashes. |
| Caliray, Beaubble, DHC, Haus Labs, Tower 28, Trish McEvoy | Smaller mascara brands named by reviewers. |

**A note on the sources.** Amazon blocks automated page loads from this machine, so Amazon pages were not loaded. Six web searches found only one line of Amazon review text (#287), plus star averages. Reddit is blocked too. When I tried to load PrimeLash's review feed (Stamped app), the permission system stopped the request. I did not retry it or try another way. Details are in section 6.

---

## 1. What the reviews tell us (simple English)

1. **There is very little independent review text about the Luxe mascara itself.** Almost every Luxe review with words in it sits on Luxe's own shops: **80 reviews** [M]. Amazon shows mostly star averages: **4.0 stars from 73 ratings (UK)** and **3.6 stars from 93 ratings (Canada)**, from search snippets (date of capture unknown) [M, snippet]. The only Amazon review text found is one line: "A great mascara that does not clump and is a pretty color" (#287) [M, snippet]. Luxe's UK shop-wide review feed was searched too: it holds no mascara reviews (its reviews are of lash-lift kits, tints, serums and other products), but 29 of them talk about mascara (8 quoted here as #279–#286) [M]. No YouTube video comment reviews the Luxe mascara: the one Luxe Short found (f4uAyWYG5dQ) has 2 comments, and neither is a review [M].
2. **What Luxe's own reviews say** (all 4 or 5 stars): it **stays put with no black smudges** (39 of 80 mention this), it makes **thin, short lashes look longer and fuller** (35), it **does not sting sensitive or watery eyes** (13 say so outright), it **survives water, heat, tears and sweat** (20: a funeral, a pool, a 110-degree day, hot flashes), and the **skinny wand** reaches the small corner lashes and suits shaky hands. Four reviews name a downside: it needs a proper makeup remover or effort to take off (2), the tube is small, and there is no navy colour [M].
3. **Where the Luxe reviews come from:** all 80 are from Luxe's own shop pages, and 78 of them sit on the **waterproof** listing [M]. So their praise for "waterproof" and "needs a remover" may describe the waterproof version, not the warm-water conditioning version [R]. They are also short: none is longer than 295 characters (about three lines; average 162) [M], so the long, story-style reviews in this file come from rival shops and YouTube.
4. **Real buyer reviews are plentiful for the rivals, and they come from older women.** Thrive Liquid Lash Extensions has **50,508 reviews on its own site, average 4.46 stars** [M]. In the 1,000 five-star Thrive reviews pulled, **695 of the 968 that give an age are 55 or over (72%), and 423 are 65 or over (44%)** [M]. In 300 one-star Thrive reviews, 172 of 260 with an age are 55+ (66%) [M]. [R] The person who writes about tubing mascara is, most of the time, a woman over 55.
5. **The number-one thing older women want is no smudging** (86 reviews mention it). They describe years of black marks under the eyes: "Every single mascara I've tried over the last 30 years plus ends up transferring onto the foundation under my eyes" (#249, age 66). The tubing type is what fixes it for most of them.
6. **The second is easy removal without rubbing** (62 reviews). “With my older skin scrubbing is a big "no-no".” (#98). An eye nurse (140 likes) warns not to rub the eyes at all; the tubes come off with water (#197).
7. **Third: sensitive, watery or dry eyes** (25 reviews describe the problem; 25 say a product did not irritate them). Many had stopped wearing mascara because of itching or tears, then found a tubing formula that did not sting (#92, #96).
8. **What goes wrong (64 complaint reviews, almost all about rivals):** clumping when a second coat goes on after the first has dried; flaking after a few weeks as the tube dries out; lashes that **droop or lose their curl**; a look that is **too thin for very sparse lashes** ("it didn't work on my 67 year old lashes", #83); smearing in rain, tears or humidity; and, for some, hard removal. Side effects caused by the product are rare in this sample (5): lash breakage after sleeping in it, swollen lids, itching and burning, and bits getting into the eye during removal.
9. **Women over 60 have extra problems** that younger reviewers never mention: hooded lids (skin folding down over the eyelid) that catch the mascara, shaky or arthritic hands, poor close-up eyesight after cataract surgery (an operation to replace the clouded lens inside the eye), and lashes that are now grey, thin or gone. Hooded lids, hands and eyesight come up in 7 reviews (theme P6) [M]. 15 reviews describe giving up mascara, or nearly giving up, before finding one that worked (or not).
10. **Price matters.** 34 reviews talk about price. Thrive costs about $26 and many say a $5 to $12 drugstore (cheap supermarket or chemist brand) tubing mascara (Essence, e.l.f., Milani, Maybelline Sky Tubes) works as well: "I don't want to spend over $25 for mascara especially when we need to replace them every 3 months" (#222, 190 likes).
11. **"How long did it take?"** Mascara works the moment it goes on, so reviews never talk about weeks to see results. The time words are about **wear time** (all day, 12 hours, 15 hours, "7am to 9pm") and **tube life** (it dries out or starts to flake after 45 days to 2 or 3 months).
12. **PrimeLash ("mascara for older women")**: only 2 YouTube comments and three passing mentions were found, because its review feed could not be loaded (section 6). Two of the mentions say it was dry or harder to remove than promised (#82, #178); one Luxe page review says Luxe was "better" (#45).

## 4. Themes, with counts

**How I counted.** Each review can sit in more than one theme. Themes marked "keyword" were counted by a script that searches the review text for set words (for example "smudge", "raccoon", "transfer" for P1), ignoring "no smudging", "doesn't clump" and similar. Themes marked "hand" (complaints, side effects, gentle on eyes, gave up) I read and marked one by one, because the keyword search confused "doesn't flake" with "flakes". [R] Keyword counts are close, not exact: treat them as "about".

| Code | Theme | All | Luxe | Rivals | Category | Method |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Smudging, "raccoon eyes" (black smudges under the eye), staying put | **86** | 39 | 38 | 9 | keyword |
| P2 | Taking it off: easy or hard | **62** | 3 | 47 | 12 | keyword |
| P3 | Sensitive, watery or dry eyes; contact lenses | **25** | 9 | 13 | 3 | keyword |
| P4 | Short, thin, sparse, pale or "invisible" lashes | **49** | 16 | 27 | 6 | keyword |
| P5 | Curl: straight lashes, lashes that droop or lose their curl | **29** | 3 | 14 | 12 | keyword |
| P6 | Hooded lids (skin folding down over the lid), shaky hands, poor eyesight | **7** | 4 | 1 | 2 | keyword, then hand-checked |
| T1 | Results: longer, fuller, darker, separated lashes | **103** | 36 | 61 | 6 | keyword |
| T2 | Time: how long it lasted on the face, or how long they have used it | **88** | 23 | 55 | 10 | keyword |
| T3 | Texture and feel (wet, dry, light, heavy, stiff, soft) | **77** | 18 | 44 | 15 | keyword |
| T4 | Ease of use: the wand (the little brush), applying coats | **59** | 15 | 38 | 6 | keyword |
| T5 | Complaints and failures | **64** | 4 | 58 | 2 | hand |

### 5.2 Thrive Liquid Lash Extensions (62)

**#81**  
**Product:** Thrive Causemetics Liquid Lash Extensions Mascara  
**Source:** Brand's own site (thrivecausemetics.com), Yotpo review widget · https://thrivecausemetics.com/products/liquid-lash-extensions-mascara  
**Date:** 2026-10-07  
**Stars / likes:** 5★  
**Reviewer:** Lynne M. (verified buyer)  
**Age / type:** age box: 55-64 · Location: Arizona; Skin Type: Dry; Hair Color: Red; Skin Concerns: Dryness, Uneven Skin  
**Title:** Best Masacara EVER  
**Themes:** T2, T7, T10, T11, T6c, T6b, T12

> My entire life I have had to purchase mascara, put it on just to find out that I react to it. It’s a lot of money down the drain. My daughter came across your products over a decade ago and she said, Mom try my mascara. As a red hair freckle face with a reaction to everything, I was very skeptical. I put it on…. and it stayed without a reaction. In fact, I wore it all day. I was absolutely shocked. I had become so accustomed to using minimal products, only when I went on date night with my husband… then washing them off within 3 to 4 hours. Absolutely life-changing. I can say as a sixty year old woman, I look better than ever because I can use products that are safe for my skin, while still highlighting my best attributes.

**#82**  
**Product:** Thrive Causemetics Liquid Lash Extensions Mascara  
**Source:** Brand's own site (thrivecausemetics.com), Yotpo review widget · https://thrivecausemetics.com/products/liquid-lash-extensions-mascara  
**Date:** 2026-08-05  
**Stars / likes:** 5★  
**Reviewer:** Corey-Jan A. (verified buyer)  
**Age / type:** age box: 55-64 · Skin Type: Sensitive  
**Title:** Don’t believe review sites that claim Prime is better than Thrive

I'm seriously obsessed and hope that Blinc never changes this formula because it's PERFECTION!

Okay, one thing: according to a Blinc CSR the Medium Brown shade has been discontinued. There isn't even a Dark Brown shade anymore. Why, Blinc? **Title:** Fantastic product!

> I am not a makeup person and I love this mascara. I bought it for my wedding on the recommendation of a friend, and am a fan for life. With other eye mascaras my sensitive eyes would water or sting, and I have not noticed that with blinc. I also love how you don't have to scrub your eyes to get it off at the end of the day. The tubes are soft and flexible, so you don't get poked when you remove them, either! I like the natural, non-cakey look that stays all day but gives your lashes extra definition. The tube also lasts a long time and I think it is a good value. 10/10 would recommend.

**Product:** Category: tubing mascara in general

**Reviewer:** @lgrillo  
**Age / type:** age not stated  
**Themes:** P2, T3

> I use a different method for removing tubing mascara which I think is better because you're not putting that glop down the drain. I use a Neutrogena make-up wipe to take off the make-up, then run the water until warm/hot and wet the wipe under the water. Press the warm wipe up to your eyes a few times, then use the same “pinching” technique you showed, but with the wipe! All the tubes come off on the wipe and you can just disard it.

### 5.15 Category: mascara talk inside reviews of Luxe's lash-lift kit (Luxe UK shop) (8)

Added in the finishing pass. Luxe's UK shop-wide review feed has no mascara reviews. These 8 are reviews of the lash-lift kit in which the writer describes her problems with mascara (straight lashes that won't hold a curl, smudging, curlers that break lashes). Raw: `judgeme-luxe-uk-shopwide-feed-2026-10-31.json`.

**#279**  
**Product:** Category: mascara in general (the review itself is of **Luxe Lashlift Set**, Luxe's lash-lift kit, not of the mascara)  
**Source:** Brand's own site: Luxe UK shop (luxe-cosmetics.uk), Judge.me shop-wide review feed, review id 49b289ed-a223-4ef3-863b-f183ea94b216 · https://luxe-cosmetics.uk/products/lash-lift-kit  
**Date:** 2024-10-09  
**Stars / likes:** 5★ (for the lash-lift kit)  
**Reviewer:** Sarah M. (marked verified buyer)  
**Age / type:** age not stated  
**Themes:** P5, T10

> I so wish I had taken a before image! The kit was simple to use. I managed to do both eyes at the same time and I am super impressed with the results! Until now, the only mascara that kept a lift in my lashes was Mac Perm Me up, otherwise, my lashes would be straight soon after applying mascara, even after curling. I don't even need mascara on my top lashes after tinting them.

**#280**  
**Product:** Category: mascara in general (the review itself is of **Luxe Lashlift Set**, Luxe's lash-lift kit, not of the mascara)  
**Source:** Brand's own site: Luxe UK shop (luxe-cosmetics.uk), Judge.me shop-wide review feed, review id ad6f7a30-c934-4eb4-922e-726e26cc3a79 · https://luxe-cosmetics.uk/products/lash-lift-kit  
**Date:** 2024-07-11  
**Stars / likes:** 5★ (for the lash-lift kit)  
**Reviewer:** Tilda (marked verified buyer)  
**Age / type:** age not stated  
**Themes:** P5, T7, T10

> I have naturally very long eyelashes but they’re super straight and downwards facing with no curl whatsoever. Normally if I don’t wear loads of mascara I don’t even look like I have eyelashes! I’ve always been a sucker for spending heaps on different types of mascara going to get curled lashes but until trying this luxe lash lift I haven’t really seen results… but omg…! I don’t even think I did it correctly as I didn’t use the lash comb and didn’t trim the pads so missed half my lashes and let me say my lashes look the best they’ve ever looked! I got the free tint with it and they look like lash extensions no joke! I love them so much and will deffo be using this from now on! Just amazing! Instructions are clear, you get loads of everything for loads of applications and the results speak for themselves!

## 6. What I could not get

| Source | What happened |
|---|---|
| Amazon (all countries) | Blocks automated loads. Not tried, as the brief says. Six web searches (finishing pass) found star averages and one line of review text (#287); nothing else. Capture dates unknown. |
| Luxe UK shop-wide review feed (Judge.me) | Loaded normally, 25 reviews a page. The feed says 4,631 product reviews, but after page 37 (911 rows, 518 different reviews, 2021-01-26 to 2026-10-25) it returned empty pages. I stopped there; no retries, no other route. No row is a mascara review. 29 different reviews mention mascara; 8 are quoted (#279–#286). |
| Reddit | Blocked (403). Not tried again. |
| PrimeLash's own review feed (primeprometics.com, Stamped app, "14156" reviews) | The page itself loaded; the request for its review feed was **stopped by the permission system** before it ran. I did not retry or try another way. No PrimeLash shop reviews are in this file. |
| e.l.f., Maybelline, Essence own-site reviews | e.l.f. uses Bazaarvoice and the Lash Xtndr page I guessed returned "not found"; maybelline.com and essence.eu are not Shopify shops. I did not go further, to stay within the no-workaround rule. These brands are covered by YouTube comments only. |
| Ulta, Target, Walmart, Sephora, Cult Beauty, Trustpilot | Not tried this round (time). Round 1 has snippet star averages for Grande (Macy's, Walmart, Sephora UK). |
| TikTok | Pages build in the browser, so no text came back (round 1). |
| YouTube comment page 2 for ORjqxk2tkMs | NexLev returned page 1 again; nothing new. |
